How does it explain or resolve that this citizen of one of the other European countries, the countries within the European Union, does not have the right to be president of the province? This is a question, although it is a logical solution to me, I will say a reasonable solution, but whether there is any constitutional or other basis for such a solution. When it comes to the election of the mayor who is direct, it seems to me that the right to restrict passive electoral rights to elect the mayor to the citizens of the Republic of Slovenia alone is more logical than when it comes to indirect elections when someone may be elected to the provincial council, but he must not be elected president of the same province. That says there's something not quite right here. And speaking of elections, I would like to repeat what I said many years ago in the Law on Local Elections, and I would now like to propose again that we might also consider this option. You probably know all those who are following discussions on provincial legislation, that the Group of Social Democrats and the entire party support the direct elections of the president of the province, that we are not advocating a solution that is in the current law, but we want the President of the province to be directly loved, like the President of the country and the mayor. Why - I wouldn't explain now because it doesn't matter that I'm here to develop a theory. The fact is that this is a crucial issue of this legislation. However, the arguments cited as the reason why it is not direct and why the indirect elections of the president of the province were particularly rooted in the problems that arise when the mayor is, in particular, because we have very long, already 12 years of experience there or now the fourth term, that when it comes to the directly elected mayor, then the problem is that political stability in the municipal world is such that this mayor is difficult to create any cooperation in the municipal world with all the parties and lists elected to the municipal council, and that he is then having difficulty in carrying out their and his election programmes in the mandate for which he was elected. That's right. However, we have cases in the European Union where there is an explicit solution to the matter without any particular problems and which ensures, on the one hand, the firmness or feasibility of the electoral programmes of all those taking place in the local elections, both the President or the Mayor and the members or parties in the provincial or municipal world, as well as the sustainability of such a link, later, for all four or in this case when we are talking about the provinces, for five years. Why am I saying this? Because both in France and in Italy, I could not say for other countries, but I know that, in particular, there is such a solution, not at the provincial level, but at the municipal level, that those candidates for the mayor who is elected, or those parties and lists who support the elected candidate for mayor, receive 60% of the seats in the municipal council in the Italian case, and in the French case even 70% of the seats in the municipal world. However, it was explained to me that this constitutionally could be problematic. I think at least if not the other one, we could open up this subject and say a more serious and in-depth word about it. This is because this other aspect of this solution, not only is it then, according to the household, to say "little mal'ca" to lead the congregation or the province with such a composition. Even if only 55% of the seats belong to the President's option, it is quite enough to normally implement the programme set up for the term of office for five years. More importantly, in the event that a dispute arises within these parties and the letters that have supported the elected President, the President can resolve the political crisis by his resignation at the moment and simply go to the new elections. Why is that good? Because it is then a kind of guarantee for the strength of the workers who will lead the province for the next five years. I think that this is a solution that is the basis for serious reflection on the different resolution of elections to the provincial council and the president of the province, and I think it would be good if the Government and, above all, the Minister for Local Self-Government and Regional Development, its co-workers and co-workers, but perhaps we will also have a public presentation of the opinions that we will probably require on the working body. I hope that she will be voted to open this debate on the law on the province's elections and ask herself if this is really such a revolutionary move, given that, let's say, Italy already has a third term, yes, a third term, and in France I think there is more mandate. And I think that this could be a solution that would be useful. And one more thing. This is about the tasks of the province, which one country or state may perform for the whole country. At the point where traffic is regulated in the province, there is talk of inland navigation. There's not a word said about seagoing. The sea is not continental water, and I therefore conclude that the intention of the proposed law to exclude the sea from the jurisdiction of the province would be to exclude the sea. There are also more debates about what we don't have on the table today than what we have on the table. We have three laws on the table: the general law on provinces, elections and financing. With regard to the provinces, it raises some fundamental questions. The breakdown of the landscapes is the most important issue, with the most questions and the most debates. We do not have this on the table today, although it is true that we are all talking about 14 provinces, that it is already some material that is prepared. And that's what this is supposed to be. Today's package does not talk about it, is adopted by a simple majority, with a two-thirds majority we will accept the division territorially and eventually also the identification of the seat and areas and the number of landscapes. What do we have today as an answer at the table? We don't have a line. As far as content is concerned, we have only a few specific powers, and in the end, we do not have all the answers regarding the financing of the provinces. We just have a few. These are essential questions for me. We do not have the answer or the province will be in the final stage of the amphibian or not, or administrative tasks that are now being carried out by administrative units as state authorities or not will also be included. Minister Virant, who has also apparently realized that unit administrations are a relatively good part of the state administration, and this also emphasises that I am also joining his opinion, took some time to think. First of all, as far as content is concerned. We only have some remittances, something more concrete, but more or less is just descriptive, content sets, what the province is supposed to do. I do not know what will happen in the field of health, specifically what will happen in the areas of education, transport and communications, etc. This will all follow, and this content is not there. Of course, it will be very wrong if this depends solely on the performance of a certain minister, his lobbying skills and final confirmation in this National Assembly. It can happen to us that some area will become even more centralized than it is today, but that functions will be transferred to the provinces to a greater or lesser extent. I think that with these answers we should come in a package, and I also expect that the Government will come up with a second reading of this legislation, which is obviously going to receive support in this package of three laws today, with these answers, with more concrete answers regarding funding, content and, of course, also partitioning itself, because this is also a decisive step in the end. But if evil is already strong enough for this to happen, of course we will follow this path. Certainly, in my opinion, it would be significantly better if we had the whole package on the table and knew what we were talking about. As regards funding as another important issue, I must say that certain solutions are indicated, but these solutions go in the direction of the dependence of the future provinces on the country, on the central government, as is now the case in the area of local self-government or municipalities. We have 210 municipalities, as has already been said, and we know that the large, large, majority of municipalities do not create the necessary amounts of resources with their own revenues and are dependent on the state budget's funding. It is exactly the same in the area of the province, because we leave behind the main mechanisms for determining the volume of the financial budgets of the provinces to the state. The State or Government will adopt a regulation where it will determine the appropriate use of the provinces, everything is to the State. Moreover, if a particular province is characterized as rich to generate more than 15% of its average income, it will receive less funding if one is fed, it will receive more funding up to 15% of that tolerance. This brings us into the regional area, that is to say, to the functioning of the provinces, a certain flat-rate woman and a certain amount will be absorbed in the state budget, as the new municipalities, they will not be set up to create as much as possible. This prevents to a certain extent the initiative of individual provinces in order to be interested in creating more revenue. As far as the question of association, merger of administrative units and future provinces is concerned. I am in favour of the province also performing the functions now performed by the administrative units. Perhaps even thinking of doing so gradually is appropriate. Certainly, in terms of rational state administration, it must be said that it makes sense for these functions to be brought together in the future and thus to approach people in a certain way. As for the separation itself, this famous number 14, but I must say that I disagree with it. Slovenia, which is already small, is much debated about its smallness, I think it will take another important step to prove its smallness also in the province's membership. 14 provinces I think are very much in Slovenia. We know what our problems are as a result of this breakdown, and I am most concerned that these provinces will not be comparable and will not be an equal partner for regional structures in neighbouring countries, which is essential. On the other hand, they will produce an unnecessary amount of government or regional administration if this happens. And I'm sure that the number - of course we're talking somewhere - is significantly smaller in the case, we're talking about three, which I think would be a really far-off version up to about 8, because it would be an optimal option. These would be historic landscapes. [[Primorska, Gorenjska, Dolenjska, Central, Upper, Lower Styria, Koroška, Prekmurje with Pomurje possible]] . I therefore propose that further reflection be made and that we take one qualitative step forward in the process of adopting pension legislation in order to make this a truly successful project.

It will continue to be necessary to ensure a stable public financial situation, including with the provinces. At present, we are on a very good path in Slovenia, with the fact that we are aware of the long-term problems that we have as a result of population ageing as well as most European countries. The Constitution provides in Article 143, paragraph 3, that the State shall transfer to the provinces by law the performance of individual tasks under national jurisdiction and shall provide them with the necessary financial resources to do so. As with the Government and the National Assembly, we are therefore obliged to ensure that funds are transferred by transferring tasks from the state to the provincial level. It will be necessary to specify exactly what tasks will be carried out at the regional level, which means that the state level will be burdened, both working and financial resources. However, I believe that it is necessary to ensure a neutral transfer of tasks from one level to another, which will not increase overall public expenditure or expenditure of the central, regional and municipal levels of the country. Otherwise, we can increase public spending, which is certainly not and cannot be our goal. There are ideas that some of the new tasks laid down in the laws would be carried out in the provinces, but so far they have not been carried out. I oppose such solutions, because this can really mean a huge increase in public spending. Given that we do not yet have precise financial assessments or the effects of the law, it is very difficult to decide which tax resources or resources could be derogated from to finance delegated tasks. In this decision, we will also rely on the experience of other European countries that know the landscapes or the regional level of public finances. However, I do not think it is that important which tax resources will be deducted from the provinces, but above all it is necessary to provide a stable source of funding for the implementation of the tasks transferred. The financing of the provinces should be ensured in accordance with the principles of public financing and the provisions of the law governing public finances and in accordance with the spirit of the constitutional provision that the State must provide the province with funds in proportion to the type and extent of the tasks delegated. Funding should be provided in accordance with the principle of transparency, efficiency and efficiency and financial optimisation of public funding management. We still have a lot of work to do, even on vacation. In the field, we get Members to ask how certain services will be implemented by the provinces, and I want these answers to be received as soon as possible. It is said that we will somehow simulate what the situation will be, say, on July 13, 2011. Above all, I think we need to understand the concept of landscapes. I remember the first local elections when we elected the mayor and the municipal council, but for a few months, maybe a year, we had no idea what to do with this congregation right now. Surely it will also take some time for the provinces to come to life and actually produce the results we want. Today, in some way, I have called on Pomurce to cooperate in the design of healthy and smart solutions. I also estimate that because, unfortunately, we have not been able to carry out a single regional project in the previous Pomur statistical region, because it is perfectly normal for mayors who logically think mayorally to sit in the world of the region. Of course, I wish the entire ministerial team great luck in finding optimal solutions, but I also think and guarantee that the New Slovenia Group will participate constructively in this. ParlaMint-SI_2007-07-13-SDZ4-Redna-30.ana.u116	Thank you for your word. The proposal to amend the law seeks to raise the evidentiary standard on grounds of suspicion of reasonable grounds of suspicion. It also restricts the prosecution of perpetrators of crimes to those for which a minimum of 5 years of imprisonment is at risk, or the offences prescribed by an article that lays down the conditions for the determination of a covert surveillance measure. It is true that the evidentiary standard for prosecution in the current law has been set lower, but the tightening of the scope of possible prosecution would result in a large and wide field of investigation of offences or perpetrators. All offences which can be detected by means of the control of an electronic communications network are subject to a lower penalty than 5 years in prison, so that the exclusion would be even greater. Procedural legislation is a very sensitive thing in criminal law; small errors can cause the process to stop or the matter to eventually fall into the water. In the New Slovenia Group, we believe that it is too often the change in criminal proceedings in articles which are of primary importance for the forecasting process is not good and may be harmful at the end stage. Inadvertent changes can also lead to unpredictable consequences, so we will support the decision of the home working body that the proposal for a law proposed by the Member is not suitable for further consideration. ParlaMint-SI_2007-07-13-SDZ4-Redna-30.ana.u117	Thank you. Mr Marjan Drofenik will present the position of the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian People's Party. Please.
ParlaMint-SI_2007-07-13-SDZ4-Redna-30.ana.u118	Mr President, Members of Parliament, Members of Parliament. In 2004, the National Assembly, in the Criminal Procedure Act of 149a, lays down the covert measures governed by Article 49 of the Police Act. The amendment also adopted a new 149.b Article, which regulated and laid down the conditions for the lawful acquisition of data on participants, circumstances and other transport facts in electronic communications. This is about the order number, the type, the date, the time and the duration of the call. The transmission of such data shall be ordered by the investigating judge on a proposal from the State Prosecutor. This area is also governed by Directive 2006/24, which imposes an obligation on electronic communications operators to keep and excludes storage of the content of communications. The Directive is intended to ensure the availability of data for the purpose of preventing, investigating, detecting and prosecuting serious crimes, and the way in which data are accessed must be defined by national legislation. The rapporteurs and Members of the Group of Non-Aligned Members are convinced that the amendment to the 2004 Criminal Procedure Act is not in accordance with the provisions of the Directive, in particular Article 149.b, which lays down the conditions for obtaining data on electronic communications. This article allows the operator to provide information to the police about participants and other data on electronic communications traffic in relation to all criminal offences, which appears to the applicant to be an extensive solution for interfering with communication privacy. The applicant wishes to amend this Article in order to ensure that data on traffic on the electronic network can only be obtained where there are reasonable grounds for believing that a crime has been committed and only in cases where they are necessary for the prosecution of serious offences or offences for which five or more years have been prescribed by law. In doing so, it is worth stressing that a condition for the initiation of criminal proceedings is always warranted for suspicion and that all permitted procedures and acts must then be used to clarify the alleged criminal offence. Therefore, the reasonable suspicion is that it is always required in such procedures and it would make sense by law to determine in which cases it would make sense to use electronic communications traffic data. In today's era, mobile phone records are extremely important and have decisive evidence power in certain cases, and in a large number of cases, these are the data that help clarify what actually happened in the criminal procedure. In addition, many crimes are unknown to the extent to which they have been committed and whether they are worse or not, until traffic in electronic communications is also verified. In particular, this method has led many times to the finding of a worse crime or crime involving many actors. The evidentiary power of electronic communications in today’s world is undeniable. The narrowing of the application of this measure would in practice mean that there would be many crimes which cannot be investigated without the application of this measure. In the Slovenian People's Party's Group, we are convinced that in this case it is not a matter of interference with human rights, as it is only a matter of transport and not the content of transport in electronic communications, which, in certain crimes, have extraordinary evidentiary power. Many crimes turn out to be worse only after this asset has been used when the circumstances of the act themselves are clarified. For these reasons, the Members of the Slovenian People's Party have decided not to support the bill. Thank you very much.

I do not think this is good for the legal nature of the Slovenian state. Thank you very much. Of course, we will object to the conclusion of the parent working body, as you may have guessed. ParlaMint-SI_2007-07-13-SDZ4-Redna-30.ana.u121	Thank you. Mr Yelen will present the position of the Group of the Democratic Party of Pensioners.
ParlaMint-SI_2007-07-13-SDZ4-Redna-30.ana.u122	Thank you for your word. Honorable President, colleagues and colleagues. According to our assessment, the amendment proposal is unfounded because we are convinced of the negative consequences of the performance of the work of the competent authorities in relation to the detection and prosecution of criminal offences, which would definitely be affected by the proposed marriage of a series of criminal offences to detect and prosecute, which would allow for the use of a traffic data acquisition measure in electronic communications. It is also a fact that many crimes are continued, repeated and accompanied by other criminal activities and the success of disclosures can have a positive impact on the effective prosecution of other serious offences. We need to be clear about the next. Precisely defined and equipped offences, which also fall within the scope of prosecutions which involve a higher level of danger or threat, are subject to the provision of the possibility to apply measures, i.e. covert measures where there is a significantly greater interference with the privacy of the suspect. However, in the case of the measures referred to in Article 149.b, the measure is also milder from the point of view of interference with privacy. It is therefore necessary to take into account, in accordance with the principles of criminal law, the clear distinctions between the intensity of the measures and thus the interference with privacy in view of the gravity of the crime. The proposed narrowing of the criminal cycle for action under 149.b would therefore have a negative impact on the resolution of criminal cases. In our Group of Members, we do not believe that we should allow this to happen, so we will support the proposal of the Labour Body.

ParlaMint-SI_2007-07-13-SDZ4-Redna-30.ana.u124	Mr President, thank you very much for your words. Colleagues, colleagues! We do not support the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Criminal Law in the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group, because we do not believe that this is a matter for harmonisation under the Directive of the European Union. The Directive of the European Union referred to by the proposers does not lay down offences which may result in the monitoring and retrieval of data from the electronic communications network for the purposes of criminal proceedings. The Directive only refers to worse crimes and the definition of these worse crimes is left to the internal laws of the Member States. In our legislation, we have so prescribed that there are worse offences for which, for the purposes of criminal proceedings, a decision may be made to control individual electronic communications, that these are all offences whose offences are prosecuted under official responsibility, if there are grounds for suspicion that it has been committed, committed or organised. This provision is now in full compliance with the European Directive on which the proposers refer. While the European Directive would also harmonise an amended provision, as proposed by the proponents, but the Members of the Slovenian Democratic Party believe that it would not be good if too narrowed down the scope for the use of such covert investigation measures, since, in individual cases, criminal offences, where it is not possible to track down the perpetrators in any other way, could be prevented from being prosecuted. It would certainly not be good for society to be able to commit certain crimes for which the risked prison sentence is less than five years, but nevertheless to commit serious crimes that are prosecuted under official responsibility, in order to remain undisclosed and unpunished. In the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group, we do not agree that the current definition in itself means excessive use of wiretaps and control over electronic communications. The use of such a covert investigation measure is still highly restrictive, only after the investigating judge has ordered the reasoned proposal of the State Prosecutor. Both the proposal for such control and the order must be reasoned and justified on the grounds and the circumstances leading to the application of the measure. Only if all of this is ensured and relevant data enabling effective prosecution cannot be obtained in any other way can such a measure then be ordered and implemented. Therefore, as it is said, Members of the Slovenian Democratic Party will not support the proposed law of the Group of Members and we agree with the proposal of the home labour body that the law is not suitable for further consideration. Thank you.

However, in criminal law theory, certain gradations apply to the degree of danger of certain crimes for the individual, society and the State, as well as to the gradation, where, under which conditions and under which the State may impose certain measures which affect human rights or human privacy in this case.
